of size at most
k+1
such that
(L,R):=(V(G)\setminus V(C),V(C)\cup P)

is a separation of
G
with
L\cap R=P
.
Thus, if
\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
is the 7-tuple obtained by adding the vertices of
G\setminus(C\cup P)
to the set
in the first coordinate of
\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime}
,
(W_{2}^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime})
is a regular flatness pair of
G\setminus(A\cup P)
of height
r_{2}
.

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
be a
W_{2}^{\prime}
-canonical partition of
G\setminus(A\cup P)
.
Let
B
be the set of vertices of
A\cup P
adjacent to vertices of at least
x

p
-internal bags of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Let
\mathcal{W}=\{W^{1},...,W^{l+1}\}
be a family of
l+1

r_{3}
-subwalls of
W_{2}^{\prime}
such that for every
i\in[l+1]
,
{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}(W^{i})
is a subgraph of
{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}\{Q\mid Q\text{ is a $p$-internal bag of }\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\}
and for every
i,j\in[l+1]
with
i\neq j
, there is no internal bag
Q\in\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
that contains vertices of both
V({\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}(W^{i}))
and
V({\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}(W^{j}))
.
The existence of
\mathcal{W}
follows from the fact that
r_{2}\geq\hyperref@@ii[@idealistic]{f_{\ref*{@idealistic}}}(l+1,r_{3},p)
and
Proposition 5.4
.
Notice that the set
N_{G}((A\cup P)\setminus B)
intersects the vertex set of at most
(x-1)\cdot|(A\cup P)\setminus B|\leq l

p
-internal bags of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Thus, there is an
i\in[l+1]
such that no vertex in
(A\cup P)\setminus B
is adjacent to vertices of
{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime}}(W^{i})
.

Let
(W_{3},\mathfrak{R}_{3})
be a
W^{i}
-tilt of
(W_{2}^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
|B|\leq|A\cup P|\leq\hyperref@@ii[@collaboration]{f_{\ref*{@collaboration}}}(s)+k+1=b
and
r_{3}=\hyperref@@ii[@philistines]{f_{\ref*{@philistines}}}(r_{4},b,a-1,d)
, by
Proposition 3.5
, there is a flatness pair
(W_{4},\mathfrak{R}_{4})
of
G\setminus B
of height
r_{4}
that is
d
-homogeneous with respect to
\binom{B}{<a}
and is a tilt of a subwall of
(W_{3},\mathfrak{R}_{3})
.
By
Subsection 5.3
and
Subsection 5.3
,
(W_{4},\mathfrak{R}_{4})
is regular.

Recall that
(W_{3},\mathfrak{R}_{3})
is a
W^{i}
-tilt of
(W_{2}^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}_{2}^{\prime\prime})
,
(W_{4},\mathfrak{R}_{4})
is a tilt of a subwall of
(W_{3},\mathfrak{R}_{3})
, and
(W_{4},\mathfrak{R}_{4})
is a flatness pair of
G\setminus B
with
B\subseteq A\cup P
. Thus, given a
W_{4}
-canonical partition
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
of
G\setminus B
, there is a
W_{2}^{\prime}
-canonical partition
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
of
G\setminus(A\cup P)
such that each internal bag of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
is contained in an internal bag of
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
.
Therefore, for every set
X\subseteq V(G)
,
{\sf bid}_{G\setminus B,W_{4}}(X)\leq{\sf bid}_{G\setminus(A\cup P),W_{2}^{\prime}}(X)
.

Moreover, since
r_{2}\geq\hyperref@@ii[@unaffected]{f_{\ref*{@unaffected}}}(a,s,k(k+1)/2)
, according to
Proposition 3.7
,
every subset of
B
of size
a
intersects every set
X\subseteq V(G)
such that
G\setminus X\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
and
{\sf bid}_{G\setminus(A\cup P),W_{2}^{\prime}}(X)\leq k(k+1)/2
.
Hence, for any such
X
,
|B\setminus X|<a
.

Thus, according to
Proposition 3.6
,
since
r_{4}=\hyperref@@ii[@differences]{f_{\ref*{@differences}}}(a-1,\ell,3,k(k+1)/2)
, it holds that there is a vertex
v
such that, for every set
X\subseteq V(G)
with
G\setminus X\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
and
{\sf bid}_{G\setminus B,W_{4}}(X)\leq k(k+1)/2
,
G\setminus X\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
if and only if
G\setminus(X\setminus v)\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
.

Lemma 5.7
implies that for any
k
-elimination set
S^{\prime}
of
G\setminus v
for
{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})
, there is a set
X\supseteq S^{\prime}
such that
G\setminus X\in{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})
and
{\sf bid}_{G\setminus(A\cup P),W_{2}^{\prime}}(X)\leq k(k+1)/2
.
Since
{\sf bid}_{G\setminus B,W_{4}}(X)\leq{\sf bid}_{G\setminus(A\cup P),W_{2}^{\prime}}(X)
, we also have that

{\sf bid}_{G\setminus B,W_{4}}(X)\leq k(k+1)/2
.
Thus,
G\setminus X\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
if and only if
G\setminus(X\setminus v)\in{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}
and
G\in\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})})
if and only if
G\setminus v\in\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})})
.
However, since
G\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}))
, it holds that
G\notin\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})})
and
G\setminus v\in\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})})
, a contradiction.

10.2
Bounding the size of an obstruction of small treewidth

In order to bound the size of an obstruction of small treewidth, we first present some additional notions on tree decompositions on boundaried graphs.

Treewidth of boundaried graphs.

Let
{\bf G}=(G,B,\rho)
be a boundaried graph.
A
tree decomposition
of
{\bf G}
is a rooted tree decomposition
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
of
G

such that
\beta({\sf r})=B
.
The
width
of
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
is the width of
({\sf T},\beta)
.
The treewidth of a boundaried graph
{\bf G}
is the minimum width over all its tree decompositions and is denoted by
{\sf tw}({\bf G})
.
A
nice tree decomposition
of
{\bf G}
is a tree decomposition
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
of
{\bf G}
that is also a nice tree decomposition of
G
rooted at
{\sf r}
.

Let
{\bf G}=(G,B,\rho)
be a boundaried graph and
\mathcal{T}=({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
be a tree decomposition of
{\bf G}
.
Notice that if
a,b\in V({\sf T})
and
a\in{\sf Anc}_{{\sf T},{\sf r}}(b)
, then
G_{b}
is a subgraph of
G_{a}
.
We define the
t_{q}
-boundaried graph
\bar{\bf G}_{q}=(\bar{G}_{q},\beta(q),\rho_{q})
, where
\bar{G}_{q}=G\setminus(V(G_{q})\setminus\beta(q))
.
Notice that
{\bf G}_{q}
and
\bar{\bf G}_{q}
are compatible and
{\bf G}_{q}\oplus\bar{\bf G}_{q}=G
.

Linked tree decompositions.

Our next step is to use a special type of tree decompositions, namely
linked tree decompositions
, defined by Robertson and Seymour in
[
62
]
.
Thomas in
[
71
]
proved that every graph
G
admits a linked tree decomposition of width
{\sf tw}(G)
(see also
[
14
,
24
]
).
By combining the result of
[
71
]
and
[
18
, Lemma 4]
,
we can consider tree decompositions as asserted in the following result.

Proposition 10.4

(
[
18
]
)
.

Let
t\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 1}
.
For every boundaried graph
{\bf G}=(G,B,\rho)
of treewidth
t-1
,
there exists a tree decomposition
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
of
{\bf G}
of width
t-1
such that

1.

({\sf T},{\sf r})
is a binary tree,

2.

for every
a,b\in V({\sf T})

where
a
is a child of
b
in
({\sf T},{\sf r})
, if
|\beta(a)|=|\beta(b)|

then
G_{a}
is a
proper
subgraph of
G_{b}
,
i.e.,
|V(G_{a})|<|V(G_{b})|
,

3.

for every
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
and every pair
u_{1},u_{2}\in V({\sf T})
, where
u_{1}\in{\sf Anc}_{{\sf T},{\sf r}}(u_{2})
and
|\beta(u_{1})|=|\beta(u_{2})|
,
either there is an internal vertex
w
of
u_{1}{\sf T}u_{2}
such that
|\beta(w)|<s
,
or there exists a collection of
s
vertex-disjoint paths in
G

between
\beta(u_{1})
and
\beta(u_{2})
, and

4.

|V(G)|\leq t\cdot|V({\sf T})|
.

In fact, linked tree decompositions are defined as the tree decompositions satisfying only property
(3)

[
62
,
71
]
.
In our proofs, we will need the extra properties
(1)
,
(2)
, and
(4)
that are provided by
[
18
, Lemma 4]
.

We bound the size of a minor-minimal obstruction of small treewidth in
Lemma 10.6
.
To do so, we need the following result (for a proof see e.g.
[
31
, Lemma 14]
).

Proposition 10.5

(
[
31
]
)
.

Let
r,m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 1}
and
w
be a word of length
m^{r}
over the alphabet
[r]
.
Then there is a number
k\in[r]
and a subword
u
of
w
such that
u
contains only numbers not smaller than
k
and
u
contains the number
k
at least
m
times.

We are now ready to prove
Lemma 10.6
.
The idea is to apply the technique of Lagergren
[
52
]
combined with the bound
on the number of characteristics provided in
Subsection 7.6
. The proof of
Lemma 10.6
is very similar to the corresponding proof in
[
69
]
for
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{A}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}))
.

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a finite non-trivial collection of graphs.
There exists a function
f_{16}:{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{N}}
such that if
k
is an integer and
G
is a graph in
{\sf obs}(\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}))
of treewidth
{\sf tw}
, then
|V(G)|\leq\hyperref@@ii[@uncertainty]{f_{\ref*{@uncertainty}}}({\sf tw},k)
.
Moreover,
\hyperref@@ii[@uncertainty]{f_{\ref*{@uncertainty}}}(t,k)=2^{2^{\mathcal{O}_{\ell_{\mathcal{F}}}(t^{3}+k\cdot t^{2})}}
.

Proof 10.7
.

Let
G\in{\sf obs}(\mathcal{E}_{k}({{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}))
.
We set
t:={\sf tw}(G)+1
.
For simplicity, we use
\ell
instead of
\ell_{\cal F}
.
We set

\displaystyle d=

\displaystyle\ \hyperref@@ii[@caricatures]{f_{\ref*{@caricatures}}}(t-1,k)+1,

\displaystyle m=

\displaystyle\ 2^{\binom{t}{2}}\cdot(d-1)+1,

\displaystyle x=

\displaystyle\ m^{t},\mbox{ and}

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle\ t\cdot 2^{x}.

It is easy to verify that
b=2^{2^{\mathcal{O}_{\ell_{\mathcal{F}}}(t^{3}+k\cdot t^{2})}}
.

Suppose towards a contradiction that
|V(G)|>b
.
Let
({\sf T},\beta)
be a tree decomposition of
G
of width
{\sf tw}(G)
and let
{\sf r}\in V({\sf T})
.
We consider the rooted tree
({\sf T},{\sf r})
and we set
B:=\beta({\sf r})
and a bijection
\rho:B\to[|B|]
.
We set
{\bf G}=(G,B,\rho)
and observe that
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
is a tree decomposition of
{\bf G}
of width
{\sf tw}(G)
.
Since
{\sf tw}({\bf G})={\sf tw}(G)=t-1
, by
Proposition 10.4
,
we can assume that for the tree decomposition
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
of
{\bf G}
of width
t-1
,
Properties (1) to (4) are satisfied.

Since
|V(G)|>b=t\cdot 2^{x}
, Property (4) implies that
|V({\sf T})|>2^{x}
.
Also, by Property (1),
({\sf T},{\sf r})
is a binary tree and therefore there exists a leaf
u
of
{\sf T}
such that

|V(r{\sf T}u)|\geq x
.
We set
l:=|V(r{\sf T}u)|
.

We set
v_{1}=r
and for every
i\in[l-1]
, we set
v_{i+1}
to be the child of
v_{i}
in
(T,r)
that belongs to
V(rTu)
.
Keep in mind that
v_{l}=u
.
For every
i\in[l]
, we set
c_{i}:=|\beta(v_{i})|
and observe that, since
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
has width
t-1
,
c_{i}\in[t]
.

Let
C
be the word
c_{1}\cdots c_{x}
.
Since
x=m^{t}
and every
c_{i}\in[t]
, then, due to
Proposition 10.5
,
there is a
t^{\prime}\in[t]
and a subword
C^{\prime}
of
C
such that,
for every
c
in
C^{\prime}
,
c\geq t^{\prime}
and there are at least
m
numbers in
C^{\prime}
that are equal to
t^{\prime}
.
Therefore, there exists a set
\{z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}\}\subseteq V({\sf T})
such that for every

i\in[2,m]
,
z_{i}
is a descendant of
z_{i-1}
in
({\sf T},{\sf r})
, for every
z^{\prime}\in V(z_{1}{\sf T}z_{m})

it holds that
|\beta(z^{\prime})|\geq t^{\prime}
, and, for every
i\in[m]
,
|\beta(z_{i})|=t^{\prime}
.
Hence, Property (3) of the tree decomposition
({\sf T},\beta,{\sf r})
of
{\bf G}
implies that
there exists a collection
{\cal P}=\{{P}_{1},\ldots,{P}_{t^{\prime}}\}

of
t^{\prime}
vertex-disjoint paths in
G
between
\beta(z_{1})
and
\beta(z_{m})
.

For every
i\in[m]
, let
\rho_{i}
be the function mapping a vertex
v
in
\beta(z_{i})
to the index of the path of
{\cal P}

it intersects, i.e., for every
j\in[t^{\prime}]
, if
v
is a vertex in

V(P_{j})\cap\beta(z_{i})
, where
P_{j}\in{\cal P}
, then
\rho_{i}(v)=j
.
Also, for every
i\in[m]
, let
{\bf G}_{z_{i}}
be the
t^{\prime}
-boundaried graph
(G_{z_{i}},\beta(z_{i}),\rho_{i})
.
Since,
m=2^{\binom{t}{2}}\cdot(d-1)+1
, there is a set
J\subseteq[m]
of size
d
such that for every
i,j\in J
, the graph
G_{z_{i}}[\beta(z_{i})]
is isomorphic to the graph
G_{z_{j}}[\beta(z_{j})]
.
Therefore, for every
i,j\in J
,
{\bf G}_{z_{i}}
and
{\bf G}_{z_{j}}
are compatible.
Furthermore, observe that for every
i,j\in J
with
i\leq j
,
{\bf G}_{z_{j}}\preceq{\bf G}_{z_{i}}
.
To see why this holds, for every
i,j\in J
with
i<j
, let
{\cal P}_{i,j}
be the collection of subpaths of
{\cal P}
between the vertices of
\beta(z_{i})
and
\beta(z_{j})
and consider the graph
G_{z_{i}}[\beta(z_{i})]\cup{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}}{\cal P}_{i,j}\cup G_{z_{j}}
, which is a subgraph of
G_{z_{i}}
.
By contracting the edges in
{\cal P}_{i,j}
, we obtain a boundaried graph isomorphic to
{\bf G}_{z_{j}}
.

Recall that
|J|=d=\hyperref@@ii[@caricatures]{f_{\ref*{@caricatures}}}(t-1,k)+1
.
Thus, by
Lemma 7.7
, there exist
i,j\in J
such that
j
is the smallest element in
J
that is greater than
i
and

{\sf char}_{k}({\bf G}_{z_{i}})={\sf char}_{k}({\bf G}_{z_{j}})
.
For simplicity, we set
a:=z_{i}
and
b:=z_{j}
.
Notice that, in
G
, by contracting the edges of the paths in
\mathcal{P}_{i,j}
and removing the vertices of
G_{a}
that are not vertices of
G_{b}
, we obtain a graph isomorphic to
\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{b}
.
Therefore,
\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{b}
is a minor of
G
.
Furthermore,
|V(\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{b})|<|V(G)|
.
To prove this, we argue that
G_{b}
is a proper subgraph of
G_{a}
.
First recall that for every
y\in V(a{\sf T}b)
,
|\beta(y)|\geq t^{\prime}
.
If there is a
y\in V(a{\sf T}b)
such that
|\beta(y)|>t^{\prime}
, then there is a vertex
v\in\beta(y)
that is a vertex of
V(G_{a})\setminus V(G_{b})
and thus
G_{b}
is a proper subgraph of
G_{a}
, while in the case where for every
y\in V(a{\sf T}b)
,
|\beta(y)|=t^{\prime}
, Property (2) of
Proposition 10.4
implies that
G_{b}
is a proper subgraph of
G_{a}
.

Let
G^{\prime}:=\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{b}
. Since
|V(G^{\prime})|<|V(G)|
,
G^{\prime}
is a minor of
G
, and
G\in{\sf obs}({\cal E}_{k}({\sf exc}({\cal F})))
, it holds that
G^{\prime}\in{\cal E}_{k}({\sf exc}({\cal F}))
.
By
Lemma 7.28
, since
{\sf char}_{k}({\bf G}_{a})={\sf char}_{k}({\bf G}_{b})
, we have that
{\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{a})={\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(\bar{\bf G}_{a}\oplus{\bf G}_{b})
and therefore
{\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(G)={\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(G^{\prime})
.
This contradicts the fact that
{\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(G)>k
and
{\sf ed}_{{\sf exc}(\mathcal{F})}(G^{\prime})\leq k
.

11
Concluding remarks

For a minor-closed graph class
\mathcal{G}
, we proved that
Vertex Deletion to
\mathcal{G}
can be solved in time
2^{{\sf poly}(k)}\cdot n^{2}
and that
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
can be solved in time
2^{2^{2^{{\sf poly}(k)}}}\cdot n^{2}
, and in time

2^{2^{c\cdot k^{2}\log k}}\cdot n^{2}
and
2^{{\sf poly}(k)}\cdot n^{3}
in the case where the obstruction set of
\mathcal{G}
contains an apex-graph.
Here the degree of
poly
and
c
heavily depend on the size of the obstructions of
\mathcal{G}
.
An open question is whether
{\sf poly}(k)
could be replaced by
c\cdot k^{d}
for some constant
c
depending on
\mathcal{G}
and
some
universal
constant
d
(independent of
\mathcal{G}
).
We tend to believe that this dependence on
\mathcal{G}
in the exponent of the polynomial is unavoidable, at least if we want to use the irrelevant vertex technique, and specially our definition of homogeneity.

On the other hand, we are not aware, for any of the two considered problems, of any lower bound, assuming the Exponential Time Hypothesis
[
36
]
, stronger than
2^{o(k)}\cdot n^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
, which follows quite easily from known results for
Vertex Cover
. Proving stronger lower bounds seems to be quite challenging.

Another open problem is whether it is possible to drop the time complexity of
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
to
2^{{\sf poly}(k)}\cdot n^{2}
for
every
minor-closed graph class
\mathcal{G}
.
We tend to believe that this should be possible. However, it seems to require to use branching ingeniously and, in particular, to find equivalent instances of
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
with a decreasing value of
k
.

As for the polynomial running time of our
FPT
-algorithms, a priori, nothing prevents the existence of algorithms running in
linear time
, although we are quite far from achieving this. Kawarabayashi
[
41
]
presented such a linear
FPT
-algorithm for the
Planarization
problem, heavily relying on the embedding on the resulting planar graph. Extending this technique to general minor-closed classes would require a very compact encoding of the (entangled) structure of minor-free graphs
[
64
]
that would be possible to handle in linear time.

We also proposed an
XP
-algorithm for
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
parameterized by the treewidth of the input graph (with running time
n^{\mathcal{O}({\sf tw}^{2})}
).
As mentioned in the introduction (see also
[
4
]
), the existence of an
FPT
-algorithm for
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
, parameterized by treewidth, remains wide open and this is the case even in the very special case where
{\cal G}
contains only the empty graph, where
Elimination Distance to
\mathcal{G}
is equivalent to the problem of computing treedepth.

A last direction is to improve the bounds on the size of the obstructions given in
Theorem 1.5
. We believe that any substantial improvement should demand novel methodologies that go beyond the irrelevant vertex technique.
